# Decision log

This log is the single source of rationale for everything under `ErewhonModern/`: code comments state only what code does, and every other record (`docs/migration/behaviour-catalogue.md`, `docs/migration/feature-traceability.md`, `docs/migration/traceability-matrix.md`, `docs/migration/final-report.md`, `docs/operations.md`, `docs/rest-api.md`) cites the reasons recorded here by D-ID.

## Machine-read format

`TraceabilityCoverageTests` and `LegacyInventory` parse this file (D-48). The contract:

- The decisions are one table under `## Decisions` with the header `| ID | Decision | Alternatives | Reason | Risks |`. Parsers locate the table by that header.
- Each row is one line. A cell never holds a raw newline (`<br>` separates parts within a cell) and never holds a `|` except escaped as `\|`.
- IDs are zero-padded two-digit `D-01`, `D-02`, …, ascending and unique; the ID cell holds only the ID. D-01 to D-49 are the planned decisions; D-50 onward record decisions taken while the files were written.
- Cross-references inside cells use the IDs `B-xx`, `T-xx`, `C-xx`, `Q-xx`, `EE-xx` (`docs/migration/behaviour-catalogue.md`), `F-0xx` (`docs/migration/feature-traceability.md`) and `D-xx`. JADE sources are cited `[Erewhon/<file>:<locator>]`, one file per bracket: `<file>` is the file's path below `Erewhon/`, and `<locator>` is one or more line spans separated by `, `, each `L<n>` or `L<n>-<m>` with 1 ≤ n ≤ m ≤ the file's line count (for example `[Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L1933]` and `[Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L2368, L2468-2473]`). Paths are written in backticks: paths of target files are relative to `ErewhonModern/`, and paths of legacy files are repository-relative and start with `Erewhon/`.
- A new decision is appended as a new last row whose ID is the last ID plus one. IDs are never renumbered or reused. Every `D-` ID written anywhere under `ErewhonModern/` must exist in this table, so an ID is cited only once its row exists.
- The `## Request contradictions` table maps each contradiction to the decision that settles it.

## Decisions

| ID | Decision | Alternatives | Reason | Risks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | All new code lives in the new top-level area `ErewhonModern/`: `src/Erewhon.Api`, `src/Erewhon.Application`, `src/Erewhon.Persistence`, `src/Erewhon.SoapClient`, `web/`, `tests/Erewhon.Testing`, `tests/Erewhon.Application.Tests`, `tests/Erewhon.Api.Tests`, `tests/Erewhon.Soap.Tests`, `tests/Erewhon.Seed.Tests`, `tests/Erewhon.Architecture.Tests`, `tests/Erewhon.FixtureHost` and `docs/`. `ErewhonModern.sln` (4 `src` and 7 `tests` projects) replaces the schema load order of `ErewhonDemoSystem.mul` [Erewhon/ErewhonDemoSystem.mul:L1-8]. Each project's root namespace is its name. `.gitignore` excludes the six output folders `bin/`, `obj/`, `node_modules/`, `src/Erewhon.Api/wwwroot/`, `test-results/` and `playwright-report/`. No project references JADE, Joob or `ErewhonExposures`; the Joob application `ErewhonDotNetConnection` and the WPF client in `Erewhon/ErewhonDotNetShop` are superseded, not ported (F-001, F-015). | Modernise in place inside or beside the `Erewhon/` folder; a separate repository; one .NET project per JADE schema (Base, Model, View, ExternalInterfaces, SelfDocumentor, Consumer). | The request mandates a new top-level area and freezes the JADE sources, `Erewhon/DataFiles`, `Erewhon/ErewhonDotNetShop` and `LICENSE.txt` byte for byte, so nothing may be written under `Erewhon/`. Staying in the same repository keeps the JADE sources readable by `LegacyInventory` and the data files readable by the seed loader. Projects are cut by responsibility (host, rules, storage, generated client) rather than by schema, because the View and ExternalInterfaces schemas mix presentation with rules that belong in one application layer. The Joob bridge and the WPF client exist only to reach the JADE runtime, which the target removes. | Target names differ from schema names, so a construct cannot be found by name alone; `docs/migration/traceability-matrix.md` maps every schema construct to its target. Nothing in the solution may reference `Erewhon/` except the seed data and the inventory reader, which `NoJadeReferencesTests` and `LayeringTests` check. |
| D-02 | Project references run one way: `src/Erewhon.Api` → `src/Erewhon.Application` and `src/Erewhon.Persistence`; `src/Erewhon.Persistence` → `src/Erewhon.Application`. `Erewhon.Application` references only the `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore` package and reaches data through `IErewhonDbContext` (DbSet types) and the ports `IUnitOfWork`, `IEntityLocks`, `IServerClock`, `INotificationPublisher`, `ISessionContext`, `ICartStore` and `ICompanyContext`. `AddErewhonApplication()` and `AddErewhonPersistence(IConfiguration)` are the only registration entry points. | A persistence-ignorant domain with hand-written repository interfaces; a separate Domain project below Application; Application referencing Persistence directly. | The JADE rules read ordered member-key dictionaries throughout validation (e.g. `allItemCategoriesByPrefix`, `allTendersByOfferTime`); keeping those ordered LINQ queries beside the rules avoids one repository method per dictionary and keeps each TA port readable. Three layers are the fewest that keep the Npgsql provider, migrations and advisory-lock SQL out of the rules. (F-001) | Application is coupled to EF Core abstractions (not to the provider). A rule could drift into Api or Persistence; `LayeringTests` enforce the reference direction. |
| D-03 | REST routes live under `/api/rest/**` (e.g. `GET /api/rest/agents/by-name?name=`, `GET /api/rest/items/by-code?codePrefix=&codeNumber=`, `POST /api/rest/retail-sales`); lookups take query parameters; JSON names are camelCase; returned items and sales carry the `[JsonPolymorphic]` discriminator `type` (`retailItem`/`tenderItem`, `retailSale`/`tenderSale`); `ResultDto` carries `statusCode`, `allErrors`, `allWarnings` and `guid`. Every JADE status code and body is kept per operation. `docs/rest-api.md` documents the routes, and the Swashbuckle `rest` document is generated from the same controllers. (F-016, B-52 to B-62) | The JADE `JadeRestService` URL scheme and JADE field names; path parameters (`/agents/{name}`); one RPC-style endpoint per operation name. | Wire compatibility is not required and the repository has no outside callers, so conventional routes were chosen. Query parameters let an empty or whitespace-only value reach the same trimming and 400/404 checks as in JADE, which an empty path segment cannot: routing would answer 404 before the action runs. camelCase is the ASP.NET Core default; the discriminator replaces the runtime class of the returned JADE DTO. | Old JADE REST URLs and payload shapes do not work against the new host; a client written for JADE must change. Names in query strings appear in request logs. |
| D-04 | REST `postCreateAgent`/`postCreateClient` return 400 with a `ResultDto` holding `allErrors` for every TA failure, a duplicate name included, and 201 with `guid` on success. 409 stays the outcome of SOAP `createAgent`/`createClient` for an existing name (C-05, Q-10). | 409 Conflict for a REST duplicate, as the request states. | The JADE REST methods copy the TA errors and return `HTTP_Bad_Request` whatever the error, and `HTTP_Conflict` is declared but never returned by REST [Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L2652-2731, L293-300]; the source governs as the frozen behaviour. | A reader of the request expects 409; `RestCatalogueTests.B60_RestDuplicateAgentIs400` pins the source outcome and `docs/migration/final-report.md` lists it. |
| D-05 | Both SOAP services run on CoreWCF `BasicHttpBinding` (SOAP 1.1, `text/xml`) with `[XmlSerializerFormat(Style = OperationFormatStyle.Document, Use = OperationFormatUse.Literal)]`, wrapped; contract and service namespace `urn:erewhon:soap`; endpoints `/soap/ErewhonWebService` and `/soap/ErewhonWebServiceAdmin`, WSDL at `?wsdl`; the 13 operation names, parameter names and `DTO*` type and field names are unchanged. The `WebServiceTCP` exposure is read as RPC/encoded (`useEncodedFormat, useRPC` [Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L1933]) and, together with its `jadehttp.tcp2` transport, is not reproduced (EE-08). `Error_Unauthorized` = 403 (and `Error_Forbidden` = 403) stay as constants in `src/Erewhon.Api/Soap/SoapStatusCodes.cs` and are never returned [Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L1139-1146] (C-11, Q-12). (F-017) | CoreWCF.NetTcp for the Admin service; an RPC/encoded serializer for Admin; SOAP 1.2 (`WSHttpBinding`); one endpoint for both contracts; the DataContract serializer. | The `jadehttp.tcp2` transport of the `WebServiceTCP` exposure is proprietary to the JADE runtime, while RPC/encoded is a standard SOAP 1.1 style and encoding; neither is reproduced because wire compatibility is not required, and one HTTP document/literal binding keeps both services on the ASP.NET Core host. The method properties are SOAP 1.1 document/literal wrapped (`useSoap12 = false`, `inputUsesEncodedFormat = false`, `useBareStyle = false` [Erewhon/ErehwhonExternalInterfacesSchema.scm:L1320-1340]) and both `.ddx` applications declare `useRPC = false`, while the TCP exposure line declares RPC/encoded; reading each exposure as governing its own WSDL agrees with the consumer proxies (`HTTP_ErewhonWebService` document/literal, `TCP_ErewhonWebServiceAdmin` RPC/encoded). XmlSerializer keeps the JADE type and field names on the wire; a scratch round trip with CoreWCF 1.9.1 and svcutil 8.0.0 confirmed DTOs, null returns and string arrays. Admin keeps its own endpoint, as JADE separates it. | The conflicting JADE flags are read one way. A JADE-generated consumer cannot call the new endpoints unchanged; the SOAP ports assert outcomes, not wire bytes. |
| D-06 | The SOAP client `src/Erewhon.SoapClient` is generated by `dotnet-svcutil` 8.0.0 with `--serializer XmlSerializer --targetFramework net8.0` and namespaces `Erewhon.SoapClient.WebService` and `Erewhon.SoapClient.Admin`, from a running API at `http://localhost:5080`. The generated `Reference.cs` and `dotnet-svcutil.params.json` are committed; the floating `8.*` references svcutil adds are replaced by the central 8.1.2 pins and `System.ServiceModel.NetTcp` is removed. `ErewhonSoapClientFactory` builds both clients from one base URL and replaces `FormWebService.zBuildUrl` [Erewhon/ErewhonWebServiceConsumerSchema.scm:L6435-6474]. Retired, not ported: the consumer forms `FormWebService`, `FormAgentClientList`, `FormAgent`, `FormClient` and their bases `FormBase`, `FormAdminApp`, `FormAddressableEntity`; the application `SetConsumerDetailsThenRun`; the `WSD_*`/`WSR_ErewhonInvestmentsService` proxies; the consumer strings `Add` and `Edit` [Erewhon/ErewhonWebServiceConsumerSchema.scm:L16-20]. (F-018) | A hand-written `ChannelFactory<T>` client over the service contracts; generation at build time; svcutil's default DataContract serializer; porting the consumer forms as React screens. | The request asks for a client generated from the new WSDL, which also proves the WSDL is consumable. Without `--serializer XmlSerializer` svcutil emits DataContract types (`ArrayOfString`, a wrapped `Body`) that do not match the document/literal contract of D-05. Committed output builds and tests without a running API. The consumer forms are JADE GUI, which the request retires; the `WSD_`/`WSR_` proxies target the View-schema duplicates of D-33; `Add` and `Edit` are used only by those forms. | Regeneration needs a running API at `http://localhost:5080`; svcutil embeds that URL in the committed files and re-adds the NetTcp and floating references on every run, which must be undone by hand. svcutil's bootstrapper reports vulnerable `System.Security.Cryptography.*` packages at generation time; the same packages also reach the runtime graph transitively through System.ServiceModel 8.1.2 and CoreWCF 1.9.1, which D-50 pins. |
| D-07 | Each JADE lock target becomes one PostgreSQL transaction-level advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`), named `<Owner>.<collection>:<ownerId>` (constants in `src/Erewhon.Application/Common/LockNames.cs`), keyed `hashtextextended(name, 0)` and taken in the JADE `lockFor*` order through `IEntityLocks` and `src/Erewhon.Persistence/PostgresEntityLocks.cs`; every wait is bounded by `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` = `Erewhon:Locks:TimeoutMs`. Step order in `TransactionMode.Own`, which opens its own Read Committed transaction (replaces `CommitTransactionTI`): pre-validate and validate outside any transaction (item-code allocation alone takes its own short transaction per attempt, D-08), so a validation failure opens none; then begin, lock, apply, save and commit; the JADE order is validate, lock, begin [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L9120-9165]. Step order in `TransactionMode.Ambient` (replaces `NoTransactionTI`): the early returns of `persistEntityInTransState` come first and touch no persistence (Delete with no model object → `true`, a type below −1 → `false`) [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L9633-9662]; pre-validation and validation then run inside the caller's transaction when there is one, so they read its uncommitted changes (allocation under `SAVEPOINT code_alloc`, D-08), and the locks and writes follow under `SAVEPOINT ta_locks`: a failed lock or save rolls back to it, success releases it, and the caller commits or aborts [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L9136-9166]. With no caller transaction, the early returns and a validation failure behave the same and need none, the lock step is skipped, and `NotInTransactionStateException` (1048) is raised at the first write, so nothing persists. The three lock outcomes stay distinct: TA lock not obtained → `$CreateConflictedWithAnotherUser`/`$UpdateConflictedWithAnotherUser`/`$DeleteConflictedWithAnotherUser` in the result, not logged; closure row lock not obtained → `ErrorOutcome` kind `lock`, not logged; deadlock → kind `deadlock`, logged. `src/Erewhon.Persistence/PostgresErrorMapper.cs` maps SQLSTATE `55P03`, `40P01`, `23505` and `23503` in one place (B-38, EE-01). | SERIALIZABLE isolation with retries; `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on owner rows (company, agent, client); optimistic concurrency tokens; `LOCK TABLE`; beginning the transaction before validation in Own mode; validating an Ambient call outside the caller's transaction. | JADE locks logical collections (`company.allAgents`, `client.allRetailSales`), not rows, so named advisory locks map one to one onto the JADE targets and keep the JADE serialisation points (every checkout on `Company.allSalesByItem`). `pg_advisory_xact_lock` and `SET LOCAL` need an active transaction, so begin moves ahead of the locks; nothing is read or written between begin and the last lock, so the observable outcomes match the JADE order, and in Own mode a validation failure opens no transaction. In Ambient mode validation runs in the caller's transaction so that it sees the caller's uncommitted writes, as JADE validation in transaction state sees the objects its caller has already created or changed in that transaction. The savepoint keeps the caller's transaction usable after a failed lock, as `NoTransactionTI.doAbortTransaction` leaves the caller's transaction alive. The handlers armed in `ErewhonModelSchemaApp.initialize` treat the three lock cases differently [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L5909-5911], so the target does too. | A 64-bit hash collision between two lock names would serialise unrelated work (negligible). The begin position departs from the literal source order. Lock waits run on wall-clock time (EE-06). A missed `lockFor*` target would weaken serialisation; `ConcurrencyTests` cover the main targets. |
| D-08 | Item-code allocation (`src/Erewhon.Application/Services/ItemCodeAllocator.cs`) runs in pre-validation, before the item is written, when the code number is 0 (JADE null, D-17). One attempt is `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` = `Erewhon:Locks:AllocationTimeoutMs` (default 10000), session-level `pg_advisory_lock` on `Company.allItems:<id>`, `SELECT COALESCE(MAX(code_number), 0) + 1` for the company, then `pg_advisory_unlock` in a `finally` block. The attempt runs in its own short transaction (Own mode, or Ambient with no caller transaction) or, inside a caller transaction, under `SAVEPOINT code_alloc`, rolled back after the unlock. On `55P03` it waits 100 ms and tries again; the third failure raises `ErewhonException(500001)`. The JADE allocation window is kept: the number is drawn and the lock released before the item insert (Q-28, EE-14) [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L11846-11897]. | A per-company PostgreSQL sequence; an identity column for `code_number`; `MAX + 1` inside the item transaction under a transaction-level lock held until commit. | `getNextCodeNumber` takes a session-duration exclusive lock with `LockTimeout_Process_Defined`, sleeps 100 ms after each of the first two failures, raises `Ex_500001_Lock_not_obtained` on the third and unlocks in its epilog, all before `doCreate`. A session-level advisory lock with an explicit unlock reproduces that lifetime in both modes, because a rollback does not release a session-level lock. A sequence or identity column would change the frozen item-code scheme (company-wide last number + 1, or 1 with no items) and the 500001 outcome. | Two concurrent creates can draw the same number; the later insert then fails on unique `(company_id, code_number)` with the database-level outcome (EE-01), as JADE fails on the dictionary key. The JADE process-default timeout comes from an INI file that is not in the repository, so 10000 ms is the documented default, not a measured value (EE-14). |
| D-09 | Tender closure (`TenderClosureService.CloseTendersAtDateAsync`) reads the due items in the reversed `TenderItemsBySaleDateCodeDict` order (unsold first, closure date ascending, prefix descending, code number descending). Per item it runs one transaction: `SELECT … FROM items WHERE id = @id FOR UPDATE` bounded by `Erewhon:Locks:TimeoutMs`, re-read the sale and closure date, award the highest offer (earliest on a tie, `TendersByOfferTimeDict`) by creating a tender sale through `TenderSaleService`, commit. It stops at the first sold item or the first closure date after the given date, counts only awards, and positions its iteration by key so that every due unsold item is processed once (C-12, EE-04, Q-03, Q-27) [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L6670-6708, L7123-7160]. | One transaction for the whole run; an advisory lock per item instead of a row lock; iterating a snapshot list taken before the first award. | JADE takes `exclusiveLock` on each tender item and runs `acceptHighestTender` in its own transaction, so earlier awards stay committed when a later lock fails; a row lock is the PostgreSQL equivalent of locking one persistent object. Re-reading under the lock avoids awarding an item another process changed meanwhile. JADE's `foreach … reversed` while each award moves the item's key is runtime-defined, so the reading that every due unsold item is processed once, in key order, was chosen. | The JADE iterator may skip or revisit items when keys move during iteration, and no runtime is available to confirm (EE-04). A lock timeout stops the run part-way with the lock outcome of D-07. |
| D-10 | Soft edit lock: `SoftLockService` sets and clears `locked_by_session`/`locked_timestamp` through the Modify path (sub-codes 1001 lock, 1002 unlock) in its own transaction; the owner is the session id (EE-10). Opening an editor whose row lock is set prompts `$ThingCurrentlyEdited` whichever session set it, the opener's own included (Q-31). Yes sends SignalR `SaveOutstandingChanges` to the editor's session, and no dialog handles it (C-10, Q-09). `DELETE` on the lock clears it only for the owning session. (F-003) | An in-memory lock registry in the API; hard (blocking) locks; a handler that saves the editor's form on request; skipping the prompt for the opener's own lock. | JADE persists `lockedByProcessId`/`lockedTimeStamp` on the object through `onModify` [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L6077-6100], prompts whenever `lockedByProcessId <> null`, and causes `Erewhon_Event_SaveOutstandingChanges`, to which no form subscribes [Erewhon/ErewhonViewSchema.scm:L8724-8739]. Persisting the lock keeps it visible to every session; the dormant event is reproduced as written (D-34). | A lock left by a session that ends without closing its dialog, or by an API restart (D-14), keeps prompting until it is cleared in the database. Answering Yes has no effect, as in JADE. |
| D-11 | The stale-edit check stays dormant: `TransactionAgent.CheckEdition()` returns true and no version column exists, so the last writer wins (C-07, Q-08). | An EF Core concurrency token on PostgreSQL `xmin`; an explicit row-version column checked on update. | `checkEdition` always returns true and `expectedEdition` is never assigned [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L9007-9014]; a working check would refuse updates JADE accepts. | Lost updates between two editors, as in JADE; `BehaviourCatalogueTests.B39_StaleEditLastWriterWins` pins it. |
| D-12 | Change notification: `NotificationQueue` (in-memory deferred publication per operation) is fed by `ChangeNotificationInterceptor` (Added, Modified and Deleted entries at `SaveChanges`) and by `NotificationDeriver` (inverse sides such as `Item.mySale` and `Tender.myTenderSale`, `peerOf` references cleared by a delete, and `ON DELETE CASCADE` descendants read before the delete in the same transaction). The queue drops duplicates on (target, event, info), publishes through `SignalRNotificationPublisher` only after commit, is cleared on rollback, and drops an `Updated` event whose target is deleted in the same transaction. Hub `/hubs/erewhon` with groups `session:<id>` and `all`; events `CartItemDeleted`, `EntityChanged {entityType, id, kind}`, `SaveOutstandingChanges`, `ClosureTick` and `ClosureCompleted` carry internal ids (D-42). A deleted item is removed from every cart before `CartItemDeleted` is sent (B-63, B-64, B-76). (F-003) | PostgreSQL `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`; a transactional outbox table polled by a worker; database triggers; publishing at `SaveChanges` time. | JADE delivers object and class notifications after commit and discards them on abort [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L14150-14224], and raises them for every object it changes, including the inverse sides it maintains and the objects it deletes through `parentOf` [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L5248-5283]. PostgreSQL cascades are invisible to EF change tracking, so the deriver reads the descendants first. One host process delivers in-process, so an in-memory queue flushed after commit suffices without a polling table. | A second API instance would miss the events (a SignalR backplane would be needed). The derivation must mirror JADE inverse maintenance; a missed relationship leaves a stale screen, which the B-76 tests cover. An event is lost if the process dies between commit and publish. |
| D-13 | Identity: `SessionRegistry` issues a random session id at `POST /api/app/sessions` for the selected user; the React app sends it in the header `X-Erewhon-Session`; `SessionMiddleware` resolves it into `ISessionContext` (role Client, Agent or Administrator; agent or client id). Cart, checkout and tender operations use the session's client. The API enforces no role checks; role visibility (menus, form guards of D-44) is applied in React. REST and SOAP stay anonymous. No credentials exist. | ASP.NET Core authentication (cookies, JWT, Identity) with server-side role authorisation; passing the user id in each request body. | JADE logon is a selection from a list: `isUserValid` checks only the selected user code and never the password [Erewhon/ErewhonViewSchema.scm:L6225-6304], and the TAs and providers check no roles. The request forbids credentialed authentication and new features. A server-issued id still binds cart and tender actions to the logged-on client, as `app.getClient()` does. | Any caller that knows the API can reach the admin endpoints or pass any name to REST and SOAP, exactly as in JADE; the stack is unsuitable for exposure beyond a trusted network without a later decision. |
| D-14 | Session, cart (`InMemoryCartStore`) and closure-schedule (`ClosureScheduleRegistry`) state live in in-process singleton registries keyed by session id. | Redis or another distributed cache; database tables for sessions and carts; ASP.NET Core session state. | JADE holds `myAgent`, `myClient`, `myShoppingCartTA`, `myShopItemSearch` and the closure timer as transient objects of one application session; in-process registries reproduce that lifetime with no new infrastructure. | All three are lost on restart (users log on again, carts empty, schedules stop), and the API cannot scale out to more than one instance. |
| D-15 | The server clock is `IServerClock`, implemented by `PostgresServerClock` as `SELECT clock_timestamp()::timestamp(3)` on the database server; the server date is its date part. Dates map `date` ↔ `DateOnly`; timestamps map `timestamp(3) without time zone` ↔ `DateTime` with `Kind = Unspecified`. | The API host clock (`DateTime.Now`); `timestamptz` stored in UTC; `now()` (transaction start time). | `Utilities.getServerDate`/`getServerTimeStamp` read JADE `actualTimeServer`, the database server's local time in milliseconds [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L14736-14753]. `clock_timestamp()` is the time of the statement, not of the transaction start, and `timestamp(3)` keeps JADE's zone-less millisecond values. | The PostgreSQL server's time zone setting governs every default date and the closure date; local-time timestamps are ambiguous across a daylight-saving change. Each clock read is a database round trip. |
| D-16 | Money and percentages are rounded to 2 places with ties away from zero (`DecimalRules.Round2`) at every assignment to a `Decimal[12,2]` or `Decimal[5,2]` value; intermediate results are not rounded; columns are `numeric(12,2)` and `numeric(5,2)`. The `DecimalPercentPrecision = 6` constant is ignored because the attribute declaration governs (Q-04). | .NET default banker's rounding (`MidpointRounding.ToEven`); rounding for display only; leaving rounding to PostgreSQL on insert. | JADE rounds only at the final assignment, ties away from zero (JADE 2022 documentation, *Decimal Type*); commission 23 × 3.5% = 0.805 must store 0.81 (B-29). PostgreSQL also rounds half away from zero, but only on write, which would leave the in-memory value used by the same operation unrounded. | A missed `Round2` call stores an unrounded or half-to-even value; `BehaviourCatalogueTests.B29_CommissionFallbackAndRounding` and `BehaviourCatalogueTests.B14_PercentageStoredWithTwoDecimals` pin it. |
| D-17 | JADE null semantics: `JadeValues.IsNull` treats a zero `Integer`/`Decimal`, an empty `String` and a day-0 `Date` as null, and every ported `= null` test goes through it. Consequences kept: item price 0 rejected with `$ItemPriceNotValid` (C-03, Q-02); retail sale price 0 rejected with `$RetailSalePriceRequired` (C-04, Q-06); code number 0 triggers allocation (D-08); a search price bound of 0 means no bound (D-37). | .NET null-only checks (`price is null`); accepting a price of 0 as the request states. | The source tests `price = null or price < 0` and `salePrice = null` [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L11804, L12990], which JADE evaluates as true for 0, and `TestRetailSale.testInvalidPrice` sets 0 and expects `$RetailSalePriceRequired`; the source governs. | A reader of the request expects 0 to be accepted. A new check written without `IsNull` would reintroduce .NET semantics; `BehaviourCatalogueTests.B07_ItemPriceZeroIsRejected` and `TestRetailSale.testInvalidPrice` pin both cases. |
| D-18 | Seed: `src/Erewhon.Persistence/Seed/InitialDataLoader.cs` ports `loadData` step for step (purge committed first, company, `dateOffset`, the ten files in JADE order inside one load transaction, post-load closure), passes every record through the application services in Ambient mode and ignores each result, as JADE does. The governing counts are those the loader rules produce: 3 categories (C-01) and 100 of 160 tenders persisted, 60 rejected with `$TenderItemAlreadyClosed` (C-02, Q-20). The pattern is anchored at the start of the text only: the leading `yyyy-MM-dd`, with `T` or a space and `HH:mm:ss[.fff]` when they follow, is formatted and any characters after it are ignored (`2024-03-07oops` → `07 March 2024`; `2024-03-07T14:05` → the date with time `00:00:00`). A text whose start does not match, or whose matched part names an impossible month, day, hour, minute or second, is returned unchanged; `null`, `undefined` and `''` give `''`. A value that is not finite, or whose scaled units reach 10^15 (an amount of 14 or more integer digits in `currencyFormat`), and a `decimalString` scale that is not an integer from 0 to 28 (the `JadeFormat.DecimalString` range), raise `RangeError`. A value below a tie is never moved onto the tie, as the 15-significant-digit normalisation did (`2.4999999999999996` at scale 0 → `2` and `1.004999999999999` → `$1.00`, where that normalisation gave `3` and `$1.01`). The integer built from the digits stays below 10^15, so it and the added rounding unit are exact doubles and `BigInt` or a decimal library adds nothing. A `numeric(12,2)` amount needs at most 12 significant digits, so inside the bound the text equals the C# `decimal` text, and the 10^15 bound raises `RangeError` rather than print a figure that could be wrong without notice. The `UnitOfWork` and `IUnitOfWork` documentation promise that a failed commit propagates. The statement asks PostgreSQL for the state it actually holds through the context's public API; the command tag is not exposed, reflection binds to provider internals that a patch release may change, and tracking in the application layer misses failures raised outside the services, such as raw statements of other adapters. | Every commit makes one more round trip to the database. Whether a default quota trips depends on how the serializer reads base64 content (with XmlSerializer a 15,426-byte seed photo was read under the default quotas), and no JADE behaviour defines a size limit, so the client sets none instead of relying on that reader detail. Buffered transfer requires `MaxBufferSize` to equal `MaxReceivedMessageSize`. A new client per call keeps the factory immutable and safe to share across threads, and matches `ClientBase` ownership. svcutil generates the Admin client from the Admin contract alone, which does not inherit the WebService contract. The send timeout bounds each request-reply call, from opening the channel to the reply; the longest wait inside a SOAP operation on the server is one TA lock attempt bounded by `Erewhon:Locks:TimeoutMs` (default 10 s, D-07), so one minute leaves margin above it. The receive timeout is set to the same value so no client wait is left to an implicit default. Explicit values keep the client's outbound wait policy stated in code. | A faulty or hostile server can send an arbitrarily large message into client memory; the client is meant only for the Erewhon host. A base address with `https`, a query (for example `?wsdl`) or a fragment is refused, not normalised. A caller that needs lookups and admin operations holds two clients. A call the server takes longer than one minute to answer fails on the client with `TimeoutException`; the client stays open, and the server operation is not cancelled, so it may still complete. The values are fixed in code, so a slower deployment needs a code change. |

The shared-component rule keeps one projection per DTO for REST, SOAP and the app endpoints, keeps `OperationResult.Guid` for the REST and SOAP `guid` fields only, and has the app endpoints serialise the result unchanged. An attribute on the shared member keeps one record per shape and covers every nested projection on every app endpoint at once; copies would duplicate each projection and could drift from it; a modifier on the app's serializer options would hide the exclusion from the records and leave the `app` OpenAPI schema listing `guid`. The REST and SOAP mappers read `Guid` in code, not through JSON, so their `guid` fields keep their values. (2) The session id is the only identity (D-13): whoever holds it acts as that session through `X-Erewhon-Session` and the hub `session` query (D-39), so another editor's id in a status body lets any session act as that editor. The prompt needs only whether the row is locked, by anyone or by the caller (B-40), and the server sends the save request to the stored owner, which no editor handles (Q-09, D-10, D-93), so no client reads the owner; a hidden, redacted or hashed member would still be a member no consumer reads. (3) D-42 keys React rows by `id`, and a cart holds one line per item id, so the item id is unique within a receipt and matches `CartLineView.itemId`; a code is mutable item data and a line index is no row identity. (4) Explicit sources state each route value's origin, as the other app handlers do, without changing any route, constraint, name or response. | Every JSON serializer of these records, a future one included, omits `Guid`, so a new consumer that needs the source GUID must map it in code as the REST and SOAP mappers do; JSON carrying `guid` read back into a projection gives a null `Guid` although the member is declared non-nullable. App consumers must not expect `guid`, `lockedBySession` or any lock-owner value, so an editor cannot tell which session holds a lock. `web/src/api/types.ts` must be kept in step with the records by hand (D-126). |
| D-146 | `src/Erewhon.Api/Cli/CommandLineVerbs.cs` handles a failed `migrate` or `seed` verb within D-64 and D-124 as follows.<br>(1) An exception other than a cancellation the caller requested is passed to `ErrorOutcomeMiddleware.Translate` with the scope's `PostgresErrorMapper`, so a `55P03` anywhere in its inner or aggregate chain becomes `ErewhonLockException`.<br>(2) When `ErrorOutcomeMiddleware.IsLogged` is false, which is the lock outcome of a tender item row lock that the `seed` post-load closure (D-29) could not obtain, nothing is logged. Otherwise `ErrorOutcomeMiddleware.LogOutcome` writes one error event under the category `Erewhon.Api.Cli.CommandLineVerbs`, event id 500 `ErewhonUnhandledException`, with `ErrorCode`, `ErrorText`, `ErrorItem` and the sanitized attached exception, inside a log scope whose `Verb` property is the verb.<br>(3) Every such failure returns exit code 1, logged or not. Nothing is rolled back: the committed load and the awards made before the failure stay.<br>(4) A wrong `seed` operand count is a usage error: `$ExampleCommandLine` on standard output, exit code 1 and no log event, as `dataLoaderApp` writes the message and returns [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L5569-5613].<br>(5) A cancellation requested through the verb's token is rethrown without a log event. (F-004, F-022, B-38, D-36) | The verb's own template `{Verb} failed with error {ErrorCode}: {ErrorText}`, with the exception message as `ErrorText` and the original exception attached; logging the lock outcome as an error or a warning; writing the `$BaseLockMessageHeading` and `$BaseLockMessageBodyWeb` text to standard output; exit code 0 for the lock outcome; redacting messages after the sink with a Serilog enricher or filter; logging the usage error. | `commonLockExceptionHandler` logs nothing for a lock exception [Erewhon/ErewhonBaseSchema.scm:L448-544], and B-38 keeps the closure row-lock outcome without a log entry wherever the closure runs. Provider exception messages carry the server host and port, the user name and row values (CWE-532), which D-64 keeps out of every event. Reusing the middleware's translation and sanitized rendering gives HTTP, SOAP, the closure schedule (D-123) and the CLI one event shape, whereas redaction after the sink would depend on recognising every provider message format. The lock outcome ends the verb before the closure reports its count, so exit code 1 tells a calling script that the load did not complete. The usage error is decided before any work and is reported on standard output, as JADE reports it. | A `seed` stopped by the lock outcome exits 1 with no log line naming the cause; the operator sees that neither `$TenderSalesBuilt` nor `$NoTendersToCloseAsAt` was written, and the due tenders close at the next admin-area logon or closure run. `ErrorText` of an exception without a JADE number is only its type name, so the cause of a database or connection failure must be read from `@x` (types and stack traces) or the PostgreSQL server log. |
| D-147 | Logout and the requests of a session that are still running are ordered as follows; this supersedes the logout ordering of D-115 part (4) and D-119 part (5).<br>(1) `src/Erewhon.Api/Closure/ClosureScheduleRegistry.cs`: `Start` removes the session's schedule and computes minutes × 60 with overflow checking as D-119 part (3) states, then returns false without starting a schedule when `SessionRegistry.TryGet` does not find the session. The lookup and the insertion run under the lock that `Stop` takes, and `SessionRegistry.Remove` takes the session out of its dictionary before it raises `SessionRemoved`.<br>(2) `src/Erewhon.Api/Sessions/SessionRegistry.cs`: each `SessionEntry` counts its leases and records whether it has ended, under the entry's own lock. `TryAcquire` returns a `SessionLease` only for a stored entry that has not ended. `Remove` takes the entry out of the dictionary, ends it, discards the cart at once when no lease is held, and then raises `SessionRemoved`; disposing the last lease of an ended entry discards the cart. `src/Erewhon.Api/Sessions/SessionMiddleware.cs` holds a lease for every `/api/app/**` request whose header names a stored session, from session resolution until the rest of the pipeline has ended, so the logout request's own lease discards the cart before its 204 is sent. A request whose lease cannot be taken is treated as having no session (D-39, B-79). The hub keeps its lookup without a lease, and `ICartStore` and `InMemoryCartStore` are unchanged. (D-13, D-14, D-30, B-66)<br>(3) `tests/Erewhon.Api.Tests/Sessions/SessionLifetimeTests.cs` replays both interleavings with `TaskCompletionSource` barriers: in process against the production `SessionRegistry`, `ClosureScheduleRegistry` and `InMemoryCartStore`, and over HTTP against a dedicated `ErewhonApiHost` whose `MapEndpoints` hook adds one test-only `/api/app` endpoint that waits after session resolution and then adds a cart line, empties the cart through `ShoppingCartService.Empty` or starts a schedule. Its sessions are opened with `SessionRegistry.Create`, as the fixture bridge opens its sessions (D-47). | (1) Relying on `SessionRemoved` alone; checking membership outside the schedule lock; a lifetime lease shared by schedules and carts.<br>(2) A tombstone set of removed ids in the cart store; a membership check before each `GetCart`; draining logout asynchronously by waiting for the session's running requests before answering; leasing the hub connection as well; a per-session asynchronous lock held by each request.<br>(3) Timed sleeps between the requests; a pause hook inside the production shop and closure routes; opening the sessions through `POST /api/app/sessions` with a company, client and item built in the database. | (1) `SessionRemoved` is raised once per session, so a Start that resolved its session before logout and resumes after the event would otherwise install a repeating schedule that nothing removes, and closures would keep running for a session that no longer exists, against B-66's logout halt. With the dictionary removal ordered before the event and the lookup under the lock of `Stop`, either side wins outright: a Start that finds the session is undone by the event's `Stop`, and a Start after the removal finds no session. Returning false reuses the start route's existing stopped answer.<br>(2) A request that resolved its session before logout can reach the cart store after `Remove`, and `GetCart` creates a cart for any id, so a single eviction at logout can be followed by a recreated cart that nothing evicts, against D-14's logout discard. A check before `GetCart` is separate from the access and still races. A tombstone set keeps every removed id for the life of the process and puts session lifetime into `ICartStore`, which the Application layer and its tests use with ids that were never registered. Draining logout holds its response on unrelated long requests and needs an asynchronous wait inside `Remove`. Counting leases under the entry's existing lock never holds a monitor across an await, and deferring only the final eviction keeps the return value of `Remove` and the timing of `SessionRemoved` unchanged. A hub connection lasts as long as the browser stays connected and never touches the cart, so a lease on it would postpone every logout's cart eviction until the browser disconnects.<br>(3) Barriers fix the order of resolution, logout and resumption on every run, where sleeps only make it likely. A test-only endpoint leaves the production routes without a test hook, and it does at the resumed point what those routes do there: it reaches the cart store, the cart service and the schedule registry with the session the middleware resolved. The lifetime under test does not depend on the user behind the session, so the cases need no database fixture. | (1) A Start for a removed session answers `started: false`, as a non-positive minutes value does; an overflowing minutes value still throws after the removal.<br>(2) A long-running app request of a logged-out session delays the final cart eviction until it ends. A cart reached for a removed id outside the middleware's lease, by a background service or a path outside `/api/app`, is not evicted. A lease that is never disposed keeps the removed session's cart until the process ends; the middleware disposes each lease in a `finally`.<br>(3) The shop and closure routes themselves are not paused; a route that reached the cart or the schedule registry other than through the request the middleware leases would not be covered. |
| D-148 | `src/Erewhon.Application/Cart/ShoppingCartService.cs` `AddItemAsync` reconciles its line with the item's existence after the insert: once `ShoppingCart.Add` has put the line in the cart, it reads again, in one query by id (`Db.Items.AnyAsync`), whether the item still exists.<br>(1) An item no longer found → this call's own line object is removed with `ShoppingCart.RemoveLines`, so a line another call holds for the same item stays, and the add returns `$ItemRequired`, not succeeded.<br>(2) An exception from that read, cancellation included, removes the line the same way and propagates.<br>(3) Every other outcome is unchanged: `$ItemRequired` for no item or an unknown id before any insert; `$TenderOfferLessThanReserve` with the tender focus field; the code-length `StringTooLongException`; the retail sale price and the tender time tendered taken at add time; and the duplicate-line `InvalidOperationException` that `ShoppingCart.Add` throws before the read, leaving the existing line in place. (F-003, F-008, B-23, B-24, B-63, B-76, D-12, D-92, D-97, D-99) | A keyed per-item async gate shared by the add and the eviction in `NotificationQueue.FlushAsync`, held from the item read to the insert; an item-id tombstone set in the cart store that the eviction fills and `ShoppingCart.Add` refuses; an extra eviction in the publisher before `CartItemDeleted` is sent; no reconciliation. | Without it, a delete that commits and flushes between the add's item read and its insert finds no line to evict and sends no `CartItemDeleted`, and the resumed add installs a line for the deleted item that nothing removes, against the B-63 and B-76 rule that a deleted item leaves every cart before `CartItemDeleted` is sent. JADE subscribes the cart to the item's delete event in `addItem` [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L13389-13391] and removes the line in `ShoppingCartTA.sysNotification` [Erewhon/ErewhonModelSchema.scm:L13840-13851].<br>The read runs outside any transaction, so PostgreSQL READ COMMITTED gives it a snapshot taken when it starts, after the insert. If that snapshot precedes the delete's commit, the insert precedes the delete's post-commit flush (D-99), which evicts the line and sends `CartItemDeleted`; if it follows the commit, the read sees the deletion and the add removes its own line. Either way no line for a deleted item outlives the add and the flush.<br>A gate would have to span scopes and awaits and would serialise every add of an item against every delete flush. A tombstone set grows with every deleted item for the life of the process and needs its own expiry. An extra publisher eviction runs alongside the flush's own and leaves the same window. | One extra database round trip per add that inserts a line. The line is briefly visible to a concurrent cart read of the same session before the add removes it, and that read shows it with an empty name and a zero price. When the flush evicts the line first, the session receives `CartItemDeleted` and the add also returns `$ItemRequired`. |
